ParlaMint-PL_2021-08-11-sejm-36-1.u11	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Culture and Communications on the draft law amending the Act on Radiophony and Television, prints No. 1389 and 1442. [[Shame on you!]] Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on 8 July 2021 referred the above draft law to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading. The Committee on Culture and Media, following its first reading and the review of the Act at its meeting on 27 July 2021, asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. 29 members of the committee took part in the vote, 16 votes in favour, 12 votes against and one abstention. During the work, the committee listened to the justification presented by Mr Marek Suski. The aim of the project is to clarify the rules enabling the National Council of Radio and Television to effectively counter the possibility of taking control of radio and television broadcasters by any entity outside the European Union, including entities from States that pose a significant threat to State security. The Commission rejected the requests for rejection of the draft law and for a public hearing. However, during her work she held several hours of discussion with many Members - not only members of the Committee on Culture and Media, but also many Members of the Polish Parliament from outside the committee - and listened to the views of several social actors who were invited to her meeting. The Commission adopted inter alia. the amendment that the amended provisions on concessions for companies with capital participation of foreign persons do not apply to entities established in a Member State of the European Economic Area, provided that such person is not dependent within the meaning of the Commercial Companies Code on a foreign person whose registered office or permanent residence is in a non-EEA State. Importantly, it also accepted an amendment stating that shares or shares in a company with a direct or indirect participation of foreign persons sold in the performance of the adjustment obligation cannot be acquired for the purpose of disposal or acquisition by the State Treasury, a public mission company, a public legal entity and a company with a direct or indirect participation of the State Treasury. In addition, the committee rejected several amendments, including the amendments by Mr Suchon and Mr Bukowiec. These five amendments were subsequently tabled as minority proposals. The Commission, of course, also listened to representatives of the National Radio and Television Council. As I have already said, the Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt a draft law amending the Act on Radio and Television. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

Before us, ladies and gentlemen, there is a great deal of work in terms of taking up many Polish aspects, and that it is built to a large extent in local governments, it is local government authorities - not only executive bodies, not only wójtów, mayors, presidents, members of the management boards of powiats and voivodeship seymists, but also councillors - they should feel that they share in this great effort to build a new Polish order. ParlaMint-PL_2021-08-11-sejm-36-1.u77	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice in relation to the parliamentary draft law amending the act on the remuneration of persons holding government positions and certain other laws, print No 1447. This project is connected with the need to realise the salaries of people who occupy important state and local government positions and is part of a new socio-economic programme for Poland, Nowy Ład, for a period of pocovidowy. These changes also involve changes in tax and wage policies in many areas of social life. It is worth noting that in the last 6 years of the rule of law and justice there has been a 60% increase in the minimum wage, the average wage in the enterprise sector by 46%, in trade by almost 49%. It is also worth mentioning the wages of people with important functions in other European countries. In 2020, the President of Switzerland made 168 thousand in Polish gold. zł, chancellor of Germany - 127 thousand. zł... [[Because he deserves it.]] [[Because he deserves it.]] ...the chancellor of Austria - 110 thousand. zł, Prime Minister of Denmark - 78 thousand. zł, President of France - 66 thousand. zł, President of the Czech Republic - 51 thousand. zł, and the president of small Lithuania - 42 thousand. zł. The President of the Republic of Poland earned at that time a total gross of not much more than 20 thousand. PLN and the Prime Minister of the Polish Government - less than 17 thousand. gross zloty. It is worth mentioning that during the period of the Second Polish Republic in 1935, President Ignacy Mościcki earned dignifiedly - over 21 thousand. PLN, when a litre of gasoline cost 68 gr, the worker made 102 PLN a month, and miners and steel workers - 200 PLN. That was the proportion of the president's salary to other salaries at the time. Thus, in order to bring the salaries of important people in our country closer to a slightly higher level, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice submitted this bill. It proposes that consideration should be given to the value of wages. increase in salaries for the financial year in relation to the previous year. The Act covers the subject of the President of the Republic of Poland, former presidents, Marshal of the Sejm and Vice-Marshals, Marshal of the Senate and Vice-Marshals of the Senate, President of the Council of Ministers and Vice-Presidents, President of NIK, President of the National Bank of Poland and other managers of state positions listed in the Act. Under this law, salaries and allowances will be changed in the local government. According to Art. 1 of this Act, the remuneration of the President of the Republic of Poland will include the basic remuneration corresponding to 9,8 times the basic amount and the functional allowance corresponding to 4,2 times the basic amount, the amount of which is determined by separate provisions of the Budget Act. Former presidents will receive a lifetime salary of 70%. the basic salary and the functional allowance laid down in the rules on the remuneration of managers of state positions for the President of the Republic of Poland. The principles of remuneration in local government are governed by the laws listed in Article. 2 for local government, in Article. 5 for the regional government and in art. 4 for the county council. The amount of the alderman's allowances at these levels of self-government shall not exceed 2,4 times the basic amount. This means an increase in the maximum diet by 69%. At the first reading of this project at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance, the Law and Justice Club submitted 6 amendments of a corrective nature, clarification of the designer's intentions, the elimination of interpretation doubts, the possibility to finance an increase in the multipliers of the basic amount due to the entry into force of the Polish President's Regulation of 30 July 2021 and the equalisation of the remuneration rules of the persons mentioned in the amended laws with the deadline (the bell) to introduce changes in the remuneration of the managers of state positions. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice recommends adopting this bill together with the amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-08-11-sejm-36-1.u133	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Health a report on the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare and certain other laws on printing No 1450. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 3 August 2021, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading. The Health Commission, after its first reading and after considering this project at its meeting on 10 August, asks the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. Ladies and gentlemen! The draft law was presented to the Members of the Committee on Health by the Minister for Health, who informed in detail about the changes contained in the draft. In fact, there are four very important changes. One of them concerns the financing of health benefits. This is a very important change, which results, among others. from the proposals contained in the Polish Lad. It is one of the first laws, which implements the assumptions of the Polish Order, namely a change of the sound of the article. 131c Act on public-funded healthcare provision, so that public health expenditure should reach at least 7% by 2027 gross domestic product. So there's going to be an acceleration of the investigation to these values, the magnitudes in the previous years. This law also states that expenditure of 6% gross domestic product will take place in 2023. As the Minister for Health presented to the Members at the Health Committee meeting, this is a very expected change which will result in increased expenditure being allocated, among others. for investments, for research, for the salaries of health care workers, which you have repeatedly mentioned here. This is a continuation of good practice, which was introduced in 2017 by a law guaranteeing a progressive increase in health expenditure. As was recalled at the meeting of this committee, this is the first law, which in a systemic way guarantees an increase in expenditure on health services. The Minister of Health pointed out that the proposed law also amended Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 6 Public Finance Act. This amendment will exclude expenditure in part of the State budget: Health and Health: Health protection in other parts of the State budget as regards the application of the provisions of paragraph. 1a-1c, 4 and 5 of this article. Another very important change, which is in this project and about which the Minister of Health spoke, is a change concerning the Act on Medical Activity. It follows from the judgment of the Constitutional Court, which held that Article. 59 (1) (a) 2 The Act on Medical Activity is incompatible with the Constitution to the extent that it requires the local government to finance health care services provided by independent public health care establishments from the voivodship budget. Therefore, the draft prepared by the Minister for Health assumes that this will not be an obligation and that it will be possible to cover the loss shown by the public health centre. Another amendment, which was presented by the Minister of Health and included in this submission, implies an extension of the current contracts of lists of healthcare providers qualified for the basic hospital provision of health care services, so-called. hospital networks. Let me remind you that contracts are in force until the end of this year. If this change had not occurred, competition procedures would have been launched from 1 October. Therefore, the Minister for Health has prepared a project which introduces the possibility for hospitals located in the hospital network by 30 June 2022. Six amendments were tabled during the discussion, of which four were negatively assessed by the Health Committee and two were positive. One of these amendments is very important, which concerns what Mr Law and Justice has put forward by the rapporteur for this amendment, which implements the position of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki on the protection of workers involved in vaccination operations. This amendment provides for amendments to the Law on the prevention and control of infections and infectious diseases. The following Article is inserted: 49a, who says that: Whoever violates the physical integrity of the person carrying out the qualifying examination or vaccination against COVID or the person assisting in carrying out the examination or vaccination which is not entitled to legal protection due to a public officer, during or in connection with the examination or vaccination carried out, shall be subject to a fine, a penalty for the limitation of liberty or imprisonment of up to 3 years. This is intended to protect workers who implement the National Vaccination Programme. This amendment was positive. Another amendment, such a technical amendment, also tabled by the Member of the Law and Justice, concerns the date of entry into force, where it is proposed that in the article. 5: The Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, except for Article. 4 which shall enter into force on the day following the day of publication. As a result of the vote, Members of the Health Committee voted in favour of a positive opinion on the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded health care and certain other laws. 18 Members voted in favour, 1 Member opposed and 5 Members abstained. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-08-11-sejm-36-1.u135	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Law and Justice Club to present an opinion on the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws. This law can now be referred to as the 7% Act. GDP. The Act implements one of the five main pillars of the Great Project of the Government of the United Right, which is Polish Load. Act 7% GDP is a guarantee of a stable and systematic increase in funding for health protection, which, as the COVID-19 pandemic has shown, is an area of extraordinary effort in terms of saving the health and life of citizens. The Law on Reference Indicators for Health Care Financial Expenditure adopted in 2018 indicated that the indicator reached 6%. GDP in 2024 and keeping it at this level in the following years. The present law as the first pillar of Poland assumes order to accelerate the achievement of the indicator at 6% - it's supposed to happen in 2023. - and determination of the path of further growth to 7% in 2027 Thus, the provisions of the Act will allow the health care system to be supplied with an additional PLN 83 billion in the years 2022 to 2027, which will result in expenditure on health care in 2027 exceeding PLN 215 billion. The Act also sets out issues relating to the budgetary takeover by the State of expenditure at the disposal of the Minister of Health - the expenditure relating to education in the health sector - and extends the powers of the Minister of Health in relation to the transfer to the National Health Fund of the subjective grant, as well as the transfer of funds in the budget of the health department at the stage of implementation of the budget, and not just its planning. The provisions of this law will allow the use for health purposes of part of the savings that will occur at the stage of implementation of the state budget. I'll refer to the other records. The statement introduces an optional legal status for local authorities which is the governing body for the independent public ZOZ, which is linked to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2019. The Act also refers to the maintenance of the so-called register. hospital networks, extending its duration until 30 June 2022 due to the safety of the conditions for the delivery of healthcare services by hospitals in the state of ongoing COVID-19 pandemic. At the first reading of the Health Committee, an amendment to the Act was also adopted to criminalise aggressive behaviour and assault on vaccination centres against COVID-19 that have occurred in recent weeks and days. It increases the level of protection of persons eligible for and performing vaccination against COVID-19 - they gain protection at the level of public officials. The amendment introduces fines, restrictions on liberty or imprisonment up to 3 years. The aim of this provision is also to ensure the proper conduct and implementation of the vaccination programme against COVID-19. The Law and Justice Club welcomes and fully supports the solutions proposed in this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

If the Parliament of the Republic adopts this law in 2024, we will have 147 billion. That's an eight-year doubling of health care expenditure. I think I've exhausted the most important topic, which is how these expenditures are going to grow and how this is going to translate into a health system. Because these expenditures are needed for many activities. You have pointed out some areas and I even absolutely agree that they require funding. The issue of partial extension of the hospital sector - several times we discussed our ideas for hospital reform at the Health Committee meeting. I said quite clearly that we are also planning a partial extension of hospitals in Poland. However, it is not a simple extension, it is not a matter of pouring money into hospitals, it is a lengthening based on a certain restructuring, on a certain change in the model of the operation of hospitals, perhaps a reprofile of some of the functions of hospitals, but certainly - and this is the most important - of preparing a sound restructuring plan. A large part of the discussion was dominated by the question of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. Ladies and gentlemen, in order for us to be clear, as there were various thesis regarding this judgment, the judgment of the Constitutional Court does not mention that the state budget is responsible for the debts of hospitals. Maybe I can quote what this sentence says. In short, so it may not develop too much. The verdict is that it's an art. Paragraph 59 of the Act. 2, i.e. the obligation for bodies to make additions to their own hospitals in the event of losses exceeding depreciation... This judgment obliges the JST, and this is the most important, to cover the net loss which is the economic effect of the introduction of rules that are universally applicable and which have a mandatory financial impact. There's no way the hospital situation is bad for other reasons. This is the case, and the Constitutional Tribunal ruled in its judgment that the State's budget should be liable for those expenses or hospital costs which are the result of the laws introduced. Like what? E.g. Laws on minimum remuneration in the health sector. Ladies and gentlemen! The last Act on the minimum remuneration of these effects in Parliament does not transfer to hospitals, it does not transfer to healthcare providers, only all the effects of this Act, and I think that you remember, we estimate them at more than PLN 8 billion per year, are covered by the National Health Fund. They are financed by the central budget, not by entities. So, in a sense, the Act on Minimum Remuneration, which imposes such an obligation, implements the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. I cannot agree with this, in many questions it appeared that this law does not implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. There was quite a lot of talk about it at the Health Committee yesterday. We introduce an optional option for creating bodies. If the creation authority considers that it wants to make up for the loss, it can do so. This is mainly due to the fact that after the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal came into force after 18 May, according to different legal interpretations of such a basis today, the formation body does not exist, and we received signals that some bodies, not only local authorities, but others would also want to supplement such a loss. Mrs Gelert asked what would happen when the hospital would use its own fund and what would happen if the local government unit did not decide to supplement it. Madam President, such rules have been in place for many years. In my view, this was the case before 2015, this obligation was introduced in 2015. I'm gonna say more because there's been a lot of talk about liquidation, about what hospitals are gonna do. I do not know if you know, but the Minister of Health oversees the high-level bodies in the health sector, institutes which often act as trans-regional, nationwide treatment centres. I will mention the National Institute of Cardiology, the National Institute of Oncology, the Children's Health Center, etc., institutes for many, many years, it has not been your concern so far. The formation body, which is the Minister of Health, cannot compensate for losses, cannot contribute to institutes and has not been able to do so for many years. I would like to remind you that the situation in many of these institutes is very difficult, but maybe, unlike some other hospitals in Poland, because they do not receive and have no right to receive such a supplement, they have successfully implemented restructuring plans for several years. I will mention, for example, the Institute of the Polish Mother's Health Center, which today practically minimized it to zero. The Institute of Tuberculosis and Lung Diseases - a few years ago a loss of PLN 8 They will then, I hope, local governments under the supervision of the creative bodies, start restructuring, start thinking about not only adding money, but actually taking the effort and actions that are to ensure further smooth functioning. The question of lack of consultation has repeatedly been raised in the questions. Ladies and gentlemen, yesterday I explained this quite precisely at the meeting of the Committee on Health, which is the timetable for the budget bill, which we have time to prepare full planning forms for the Ministry of Finance. But it's important to be clear that we didn't break the bill on the joint government and government committee. The Council of Ministers in Poland has long been entitled to adopt legal acts, to laws whose importance is high, and I don't think we will all refuse in this Chamber that the importance of this law is high. It has the right to such a procedure, i.e. a procedure without public consultation or missing certain stages. The Council of Ministers, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure, adopted such a procedure and only because, and not for other reasons, this law was not in consultation. Ladies and gentlemen, I think 99% laws proceded by the Minister for Health go to public consultation and this is one of the few exceptions when such consultations are not held. Today, there are no limits to seeing a specialist's doctor. We've lifted the limits in pediatrics before. It's an action related to the Medical Fund Act. Even earlier, limits to four of the most frequently chosen specialties were removed, to which there are large queues: cardiology, in particular endocrinology. Even earlier, the limits for imaging studies such as CT scans, MRIs, earlier or even at the same time, concerned cataracts, endoprosoplasty, where the queues were really (the bell) long. These are actions... I'm almost done, Marshal, one minute. These are actions that also need a stable funding system. These actions would not have been possible without a progressive increase in health expenditure. I think that this catalogue of activities we talked about -- was about psychiatry, child psychiatry -- some systemic support that we started on, and investment support in these areas is extremely important. I think that such important areas - not necessarily related to benefits - like training of medical staff, like teaching infrastructure, which today is lacking in Polish medical universities and we are not able to increase the limits... I would like to remind you that the limits for specialist doctors have increased by 50% over the last few years from around 6.5 thousand. to 9,5,000. The recently reviewed regulation of the Minister of Health again increases these limits by more than 650 places to actually ensure the comfort of medical staff in a few years' time. It's a long-term operation. We certainly need resources for all these actions and this law gives the Minister of Health tangible, guaranteed measures. So, ladies and gentlemen, please, and in fact, I urge you to support this bill. As President Latos said, this law should be passed beyond divisions. In my view, it does not cause controversy, but merely brings good systemic action for this Minister of Health and for any subsequent one who will have the pleasure of managing this area. Thank you. Of course, we all know the provisions of the Act on the National Bank of Poland, which state that banknotes and coins issued by the National Bank of Poland are legal tender. On the other hand, the legal analyses carried out in the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland, conducted at the National Bank of Poland, in the Ministry of Finance, show that it is possible, despite such regulation in the Act on the NBP today, that, under private law, directing the offer to a particular circle of recipients, i.e. only those who wish to pay a non-cash payment instrument, exclude the possibility of concluding a transaction or settlement in respect of payment for services or goods in cash. Is it possible to circumvent these NBP laws? Is it possible that an entrepreneur, if he puts out such information that there are only non-cash payments, will lead to such a result that 4.5 million people who do not have any non-cash payment instruments will be excluded from this trading? And that's the need, and that's the answer. also to your questions or doubts as to whether such regulation is needed at all. Such regulation is necessary, fills an important legal gap and such regulation is also the result of an in-depth reflection, which was carried out by, as I say, the legal services of the NBP. This request was made. This presidential initiative originally prepared in the National Bank of Poland also received a positive opinion of the European Central Bank. Similar, as was the case here in the questions, the findings are the subject of the European Commission's activity, because from many different indications we should act to keep the cash turnover in cash as well. The key is what the rapporteur was talking about here, as Mr Sylvester Tułaev also said on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, that we are talking about freedom of choice for the consumer. The consumer decides the form of payment. Under the Payment Services Act, we are making a change that concerns the merchant. The acceptable one is the one who uses the payment terminal. If the consumer is reported to the trader, then up to that limit which represents an amount equal to the equivalent of the average remuneration in the enterprise sector without payment of prizes from profits in the third quarter of the previous year announced by the CSO, the merchant will not be able to refuse to accept the payment in cash. So this is a guarantee, according to today's values, of a threshold of PLN 5370.64, if this law were to enter into force today, a threshold which is to ensure that, as consumers, we can, also in the case of a acceptor who said earlier: I do not accept cash payments, say: there is a legal obligation and as a consumer I have freedom of choice, I decide how I pay for the services I provide or the goods I buy. This is a significant change of law and this is a significant change of law which is part of the absolutely modern trends that today are in legislation - we point out this in the justification of this bill - Western European, American legislation at state level, at federal level. Such a need to maintain cash flows is the subject of reflection by central banks in Western Europe, in the countries of the world. And legal solutions such as the President's bill are being introduced there. So there is no doubt here that it is not a trifle that it is not irrelevant that it is a thing which - in turn - directly affects 4.5 million Polish citizens who will not be digitally excluded as a result of this change of law. That's the first result. Secondly, it is a very important law for the future. Because these are the experiences in extreme cases, as also mentioned in the explanatory memorandum of the Swedish model, in which, at a very low level of cash turnover, the State is today taking all measures and measures to restore this cash turnover as a result of central bank intervention. Such solutions exist in Europe today, for example. that it cares about the ATM network from the perspective of the central bank or the state in order to allow citizens access to cash. And the contexts of security, cybersecurity, the preservation of cash trading are the contexts that are also important here. However, for the President, it is quite crucial that this is another pro-consumer act that fits into such a trend of pro-consumer laws. Let me just remind you that the President has submitted to the High House and it is the law in force that we have eliminated the exception to the prohibition of anotocism, which is the possibility of investigating interest on late interest only because it was the status of the bank's financial institution. This is a change of law which took place as a result of the initiative of the President of the Republic of Poland. Contrary to what happened here in one speech, the president, of course, also addressed the problems of borrowers in financial difficulties - including borrowers who have loans denominated or indexed in foreign currencies or gold loans. I am talking here about the bill, about the support, about the Creditor Support Fund, which was also adopted by the High House and later became the law in force. So a certain tradition is that the president is taking up such pro-consumer challenges. The question here was also whether the matter was discussed with the business community. Of course I do. This matter was discussed during bilateral discussions or meetings, but also at a committee meeting, a subcommittee with the business community. And I would also like to say here, from the perspective of non-cash trading, that in no way is this law aimed at non-cash trading. It's not antagonistic. These legal solutions, which today are related to Poland's non-cash or announced as part of the package of Polish Load laws, are complementary. We are talking about the situation of regulating the consumer's right to choose when it comes to the form of payment, without denying the actions that are being taken in relation to the development of non-cash trading. I said that as regards the question of the doubts that you are reporting, the need for this regulation or the reference to the article. 31, 32 of the NBP Act, unfortunately here these doubts occur, because in practice it happened that respondents met with a refusal to accept the cash payment. This has also contributed to incorrect or false information at the start of the pandemic on the issue of the transfer of Coronavirus by banknotes. We know - in this respect there were communications, information - that such a threat in practice does not exist or is minimal. These are official communications, which have also been presented by the relevant professionals. On the other hand, we are talking about the fact that this regulation is not only justified by the pandemic situation or by the unitary situation of refusing to accept cash payments, but also by system. Therefore, I thank you very much for these clear voices of support, which in all the club speeches here, with regard to the adoption of this law by the High House. For me, there is no doubt that these are good solutions, that the law was formulated correctly, and the exceptions indicated here regarding the activities carried out on the Internet, in the place of operation without the presence of staff, concerning the mass event or precisely the transaction limit that I referred to were also very carefully considered and verified by these circumstances. It is not an assumption of this law that any situation hindering the activity already carried out today, such as, for example, Internet activity, where we will face a problem-free situation of digital payment, because we can assume that if someone makes an order, settles a transaction on the Internet, he will also be able to make a non-cash payment. It is similar if we are talking about already functioning maintenance-free points. We therefore do not want to inhibit those mechanisms which are already linked to the market activities of entrepreneurs. We just want to ensure a proper place for cash in circulation; this is justified by the public interest or the interests of the consumer. This is also related to analytical work, which is carried out within the National Bank of Poland. I therefore thank you very much for your vote in favour of the Presidential bill amending the Payment Services Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-08-11-sejm-36-1.u307	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Truism is the statement that the history of Poland of the 20th century was difficult and complicated. It's full of corners, mysteries, defeats, but also victories. If we were to seek a valuable perspective, one of the most important events would surely be to regain independence in November 1918 and to create the foundations of a modern state. However, the entire effort of many generations, the victim of national uprisings and the fight against extortion in the 19th century would have been wasted if Lenin's Red Star army had succeeded in defeating the young Polish Army in the battle at the pre-wars of Warsaw in August 1920. This battle, wrongly named by political opponents of Marshal Piłsudski by the miracle of the Vistula, was recognized by British politician and diplomat Edgard Vincent as 18. A decisive battle in the history of mankind. He placed her in the midst of events like the Marathon Battle or the Waterloo Battle. Well, there's no reason to be surprised. The consequences of the Soviet victory in the Battle of Warsaw would be terrible. Vincent himself wrote about them: The modern history of civilization knows little of events with meaning greater than the battle of Warsaw in 1920. He doesn't know a single one that would be less appreciated. If the Battle of Warsaw had ended with the victory of the Bolsheviks, there would have been a turning point in history. Undoubtedly, with the collapse of Warsaw, Central Europe would become an opening for propaganda and for the communist and Soviet invasion. In many historical situations, Poland was the premier of Europe against the Asian invasion, the Tartar invasion. At no time at all the merit of Poland was greater, in no danger was more dangerous. It can be said without a doubt that Poles saved Europe in August 1920, and today this one seems to be unaware of this, as witnessed, for example. Nord Stream 2. This important battle was also fought by piotrkows and other inhabitants of the Piotrków land. The resources of the Piotrków State Archive contain invaluable evidences of their courage and love for their homeland. These are letters of volunteers who enlisted in voluntary formations to defend their homeland. Among them were both peasants, workers of various professions, as well as representatives of intelligence, teachers, lawyers, doctors and employees. In one of the piotrków temples, in the crumb of the church at St. Jack and St. Dorothy has several dozen epitaphs dedicated to these piotrkowians who lost their lives in the Polish-Bolshevik war, offering the highest sacrifice to their homeland - a tribute to life. The battle of Warsaw in 1920 together with the legend of the Legions of Józef Piłsudski became a kind of founding myth of the Second Republic. It was the one which was referred to during numerous national celebrations of the interwar period of Poland.
